$8 \times 3 - 10 + 6$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: Using BIDMAS
,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 8 \times 3 - 10 + 6 \\\\
&=  24 - 10 + 6 \\\\
&= 14 + 6 \\\\
&= 20
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20$

</div>
</div>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 8 \times 3 - 10 + 6 \\\\
&=  24 - 10 + 6 \\\\
&= 14 + 6 \\\\
&= 20
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$72 \div 9 \times 7$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: Using BIDMAS
,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{72}{9} \\\\
&= \dfrac{9\times 8 \times 7}{9} \\\\
&= \dfrac{\cancel{9}\times 8 \times 7}{\cancel{9}} \\\\
&= 8 \times 7 \\\\
&= 56
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

&= \dfrac{\cancel{9}\times 8 \times 7}{\cancel{9}} \\\\
&= 8 \times 7 \\\\
&= 56
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$12 \ \text{m} + 37 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
&= 12 \ \text{m} + 37 \ \text{cm} \\\\
&= 12 \times 100 \ \text{cm} + 37 \ \text{cm} \\\\
&= 1200 + 37  \\\\
&= 1237 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1237 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 12 \ \text{m} + 37 \ \text{cm} \\\\
&= 12 \times 100 \ \text{cm} + 37 \ \text{cm} \\\\
&= 1200 + 37  \\\\
&= 1237 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

$6.3 \ \text{km} - 140 \ \text{m}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
&= 6.3 \ \text{km} - 140 \ \text{m} \\\\
&= 6.3 \times 1000 \ \text{m} - 140 \ \text{m} \\\\
&= 6300 - 140 \\\\
&= 6160 \ \text{m} \\\\
&= 6160 \times 100 \ \text{cm} \\\\
&= 616000 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $p * 4 = 48$ find the value of $p$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
p * 4                   &= 48  \\\\
p^2 + (3 \times {4})    &= 48 \\\\
p^2 + 12                &= 48 \\\\
p^2                     &= 48 - 12 \\\\
p^2                     &= 36 \\\\
p                       &= \sqrt{36} \\\\
p                       &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
p * 4                   &= 48  \\\\
p^2 + (3 \times {4})    &= 48 \\\\
p^2 + 12                &= 48 \\\\
p^2                     &= 48 - 12 \\\\
p^2                     &= 36 \\\\
p                       &= \sqrt{36} \\\\
p                       &= 6
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $2 * q = 25$ find the value of $q$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
2 * q                &= 25  \\\\
2^2 + (3 \times q)   &= 25 \\\\
4 + 3q               &=  25 \\\\
3q                   &= 25 - 4 \\\\
q                    &= \dfrac{21}{3} \\\\
q                    &= \dfrac{7 \times{3}}{3} \\\\
q                    &= \dfrac{7 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel3} \\\\
q                    &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
2 * q                &= 25  \\\\
2^2 + (3 \times q)   &= 25 \\\\
4 + 3q               &=  25 \\\\
3q                   &= 25 - 4 \\\\
q                    &= \dfrac{21}{3} \\\\
q                    &= \dfrac{7 \times{3}}{3} \\\\
q                    &= \dfrac{7 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel3} \\\\
q                    &= 7
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $s * t = 10$ where $s$ and $t$ are positive whole numbers, what are 
the possible values of $s$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
s * t           &= 10 \\\\
s ^ 2 + 3t      &= 10
\end{aligned}
$


We can easily infer the values by using trial and error.

A quick check shows that $1, 3$ and $2, 2$ meets the criteria above.

Let's verify for $1, 3$.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= s ^ 2 + 3t \\\\
&= 1 ^ 2 + 3 \times 3 \\\\
&= 1  + 9 \\\\
&= 10
\end{aligned}
$

Let's verify for $2, 2$.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= s ^ 2 + 3t \\\\
&= 2 ^ 2 + 3 \times 2 \\\\
&= 4  + 6 \\\\
&= 10
\end{aligned}
$

Possible values for $s = 1, 2, 3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1, 2, 3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
s * t           &= 10 \\\\
s ^ 2 + 3t      &= 10
\end{aligned}
$


We can easily infer the values by using trial and error.

A quick check shows that $1, 3$ and $2, 2$ meets the criteria above.

Let's verify for $1, 3$.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= s ^ 2 + 3t \\\\
&= 1 ^ 2 + 3 \times 3 \\\\
&= 1  + 9 \\\\
&= 10
\end{aligned}
$

Let's verify for $2, 2$.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= s ^ 2 + 3t \\\\
&= 2 ^ 2 + 3 \times 2 \\\\
&= 4  + 6 \\\\
&= 10
\end{aligned}
$

Possible values for $s = 1, 2, 3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1, 2, 3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-AEBABE3E-F196-4C58-B465-E9AE5604FC6A'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Change $45 \%$ to a fraction in its simplest form.

- $\dfrac{19}{25}$
- $\dfrac{35}{50}$
- $\dfrac{75}{100}$
- $\dfrac{9}{20}$
- $\dfrac{1}{4}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 45\% \\\\
&= \dfrac{45}{100} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{9 \times 5}{20 \times 5} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{9 \times \cancel 5}{20\times \cancel 5} \\\\
&=   \dfrac{9}{20} 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>D</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac{9}{20}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 45\% \\\\
&= \dfrac{45}{100} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{9 \times 5}{20 \times 5} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{9 \times \cancel 5}{20\times \cancel 5} \\\\
&=   \dfrac{9}{20} 
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>D</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac{9}{20}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-1E0CB113-B157-4C80-BF97-74BDBC174E3F'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Convert $5300 \ \text{grams}$ to kilograms.


- $0.053 \ \text{kg}$
- $0.53 \ \text{kg}$
- $530 \ \text{kg}$
- $5.3 \ \text{kg}$
- $53 \ \text{kg}$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&=  5300 \ \text{grams} \\\\
&=  \dfrac {5300} {1000} \ \text{kilograms} \\\\
&=  5.3 \ \text{kilograms}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>D</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$5.3 \ \ \text{kilograms}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&=  5300 \ \text{grams} \\\\
&=  \dfrac {5300} {1000} \ \text{kilograms} \\\\
&=  5.3 \ \text{kilograms}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>D</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$5.3 \ \ \text{kilograms}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-9223B5BC-2F8C-43A9-8FF7-B370A90FF796'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

$0.6 \times 7.12 =$

- $4.272$
- $42.2$
- $0.472$
- $21.6$
- $2.16$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: Let's first multiply the numbers ignoring the decimal points.


$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
       &    7   &   1   &   2 \\
\times &        &       &   6 \\
\hline
4      &    2   &   7   &   2
\end{array}\\\\
$

Between the two numbers, the total number of digits after the decimal point is three.

Let's account for the decimal point now. 

$= 4.272$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>A</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$4.272$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_multiply_when_decimal_points_note

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
       &    7   &   1   &   2 \\
\times &        &       &   6 \\
\hline
4      &    2   &   7   &   2
\end{array}\\\\
$

Between the two numbers, the total number of digits after the decimal point is three.

Let's account for the decimal point now. 

$= 4.272$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>A</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4.272$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-66123310-D9F1-45C3-86F1-7A120C1FCB10'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

$\dfrac{3}{4} + 0.35$ 

- $\dfrac{6}{5}$
- $\dfrac{11}{10}$
- $\dfrac{65}{4}$
- $\dfrac{1}{10}$
- $\dfrac{4}{5}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{3}{4} + 0.35 \\\\
&= 0.75 + 0.35 \\\\
&= 1.1 \\\\
&= \dfrac{11}{10}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac{11}{10}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{3}{4} + 0.35 \\\\
&= 0.75 + 0.35 \\\\
&= 1.1 \\\\
&= \dfrac{11}{10}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac{11}{10}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-F25E82C3-2612-4DD6-9E94-BBA4771C38C6'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

What is the difference between the smallest $3$-digit number and the largest  $4$-digit number?

- $1$
- $900$
- $1001$
- $9998$
- $9899$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Largest}\ 4 \ \text{digit number}     &= 9999 \\\\
\text{Smallest}\ 3 \ \text{digit number}    &= 100 \\\\
\text{Difference}                           &= 9999 - 100\\\\
                                            &= 9899
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>e</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$9899$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Largest}\ 4 \ \text{digit number}     &= 9999 \\\\
\text{Smallest}\ 3 \ \text{digit number}    &= 100 \\\\
\text{Difference}                           &= 9999 - 100\\\\
                                            &= 9899
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>e</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9899$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-911643A5-7EB3-455D-92E5-DB7CBE6ED079'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Convert $340 \ \text{millimetres}$ to metres.

- $3.4 \ \text{m}$
- $0.34 \ \text{m}$ 
- $34 \ \text{m}$
- $0.034 \ \text{m}$
- $0.0034 \ \text{m}$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&=  340 \ \text{millimetres} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{340}{1000} \ \text{m} \\\\
&=  0.34 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>D</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$0.34 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&=  340 \ \text{millimetres} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{340}{1000} \ \text{m} \\\\
&=  0.34 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>D</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$0.34 \ \text{m}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-516A8256-4FAD-4AF0-A7CC-7D00BB6B5147'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

How many prime numbers are there between $20$ and $30$?

- $0$
- $1$
- $2$
- $3$
- $4$

</div>
<div class='workings'>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-A4713302-D07D-4F6B-A90F-15306CAD8332'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

$3.2 \times 2.6 =$

- $8.32$
- $6.12$
- $9.12$
- $6.32$
- $5.12$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: Let's first multiply the numbers ignoring the decimal points.


$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
                      & 3     & 2     \\
{}\times              & 2     & 6     \\
\hline 
                1     & 9     & 2     \\
                6     & 4     & 0     \\
\hline 
                8     & 3     & 2     \\
\end{array}
$

Between the two numbers, the total number of digits after the decimal point is two.

Let's account for the decimal point now. 

$ = 8.32$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>a</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$8.32$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_multiply_when_decimal_points_note

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
                      & 3     & 2     \\
{}\times              & 2     & 6     \\
\hline 
                1     & 9     & 2     \\
                6     & 4     & 0     \\
\hline 
                8     & 3     & 2     \\
\end{array}
$

Between the two numbers, the total number of digits after the decimal point is two.

Let's account for the decimal point now. 

$ = 8.32$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>a</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8.32$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-E67458AA-6552-4F44-B7F8-5DEBC4A8A336'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

$\dfrac{2}{5} + \dfrac{3}{5} - \dfrac{1}{10}$

- $\dfrac{6}{5}$
- $\dfrac{9}{10}$
- $\dfrac{4}{10}$
- $\dfrac{3}{10}$
- $\dfrac{4}{5}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&=  \dfrac{2}{5} + \dfrac{3}{5} - \dfrac{1}{10} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{2 \times 2}{5 \times 2 } + \dfrac{3 \times 2}{5 \times 2 } - \dfrac{1}{10} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{4}{10} + \dfrac{6}{10} - \dfrac{1}{10} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{4 + 6 - 1} {10} \\\\
&=   \dfrac{9}{10}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac{9}{10}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&=  \dfrac{2}{5} + \dfrac{3}{5} - \dfrac{1}{10} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{2 \times 2}{5 \times 2 } + \dfrac{3 \times 2}{5 \times 2 } - \dfrac{1}{10} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{4}{10} + \dfrac{6}{10} - \dfrac{1}{10} \\\\
&=  \dfrac{4 + 6 - 1} {10} \\\\
&=   \dfrac{9}{10}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac{9}{10}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-3CCC8580-4989-4891-ADF4-6BD2F0008C55'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Find two numbers which when multiplied together make a hundred. Neither of the two numbers can have the digit zero.

- $33, 67$
- $29, 17$
- $25, 4$
- $50, 2$  
- $11, 9$  

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question and options given,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{A} &= 33 \times 67 \\\\
         &= \red
{2211} \neq 100 \\\\
\text{B} &=29 \times 17 \\\\
         &= \red
{493} \neq 100 \\\\
\text{C} &=25 \times 4\\\\  
         &= \green
{100} \\\\
\text{D} &=50 \times 2 \\\\ 
         &= \green
{100} \\\\
\text{E} &=11 \times 9 \\\\ 
         &= \red
{99} \neq 100
\end{aligned}
$

Both options C and D multiply to $100$. But niether of the numbers should have digit $0$.

Answer $= $ Option C

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>C</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$25, 4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question and options given,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{A} &= 33 \times 67 \\\\
         &= \rtq_katex_color_wrong_answer{2211} \neq 100 \\\\
\text{B} &=29 \times 17 \\\\
         &= \rtq_katex_color_wrong_answer{493} \neq 100 \\\\
\text{C} &=25 \times 4\\\\  
         &= \rtq_katex_color_answer{100} \\\\
\text{D} &=50 \times 2 \\\\ 
         &= \rtq_katex_color_answer{100} \\\\
\text{E} &=11 \times 9 \\\\ 
         &= \rtq_katex_color_wrong_answer{99} \neq 100
\end{aligned}
$

Both options C and D multiply to $100$. But niether of the numbers should have digit $0$.

Answer $= $ Option C
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>C</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$25, 4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-F1897CC8-FE90-4E2E-8BE9-4A13D3D30B54'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Find two numbers which when multiplied together make a thousand.
Once again, neither of the two numbers can have the digit zero in them.

- $100,10$
- $47,53$  
- $125, 8$   
- $99,111$  
- $25, 14$  

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question and options given,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{A} &= 100 \times 10 \\\\
         &= \green
{1000} \\\\
\text{B} &=47 \times 53 \\\\
         &= \red
{2491} \neq 1000 \\\\
\text{C} &=125 \times 8\\\\  
         &= \green
{1000}  \\\\
\text{D} &=99 \times 111 \\\\ 
         &= \red
{999} \neq 1000 \\\\
\text{E} &=25 \times 14 \\\\ 
         &= \red
{350} \neq 1000
\end{aligned}
$

Both options C and A multiply to $1000$. But Since, niether of the numbers should have digit $0$.

Answer $= $ Option C

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>C</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$125, 8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question and options given,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{A} &= 100 \times 10 \\\\
         &= \rtq_katex_color_answer{1000} \\\\
\text{B} &=47 \times 53 \\\\
         &= \rtq_katex_color_wrong_answer{2491} \neq 1000 \\\\
\text{C} &=125 \times 8\\\\  
         &= \rtq_katex_color_answer{1000}  \\\\
\text{D} &=99 \times 111 \\\\ 
         &= \rtq_katex_color_wrong_answer{999} \neq 1000 \\\\
\text{E} &=25 \times 14 \\\\ 
         &= \rtq_katex_color_wrong_answer{350} \neq 1000
\end{aligned}
$

Both options C and A multiply to $1000$. But Since, niether of the numbers should have digit $0$.

Answer $= $ Option C
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>C</code></pre>
</div>

\end{aligned}
$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-A2AB9E52-5757-48BA-893F-644438CCA57D'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Joey has the six number cards shown below

$
\huge {
\boxed {1} 
\quad \boxed {2} 
\quad \boxed {3} 
\quad \boxed {4}
\quad \boxed {5}
\quad \boxed {8}
}
$

The number cards are to be placed next to each other to form different numbers.

For example using three of the cards you could make two hundred and fifty one like this: 

$
\huge {
\boxed {2} 
\ \boxed {5} 
\ \boxed {1}
}
$


</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-B0ADB99B-D433-410E-B575-35DB1355EBAE'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

If the number $4567$ is multiplied by $5$ and then multiplied by $2$, what is the final answer?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
& = 4567 \times 5 \times 2 \\\\
& = 4567 \times 10 \\\\
& = 45670
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$45670$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
& = 4567 \times 5 \times 2 \\\\
& = 4567 \times 10 \\\\
& = 45670
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$45670$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If the number $40505$ is divided by $25$ and then multiplied by $5$, what is the final answer?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {40505} {25} \times 5 \\\\
&= \dfrac {40505 \times 5} {25} \\\\
&= \dfrac {8101 \times 5 \times 5} {5 \times 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {8101 \times \cancel {5} \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5} \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
&= 8101
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8101$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {40505} {25} \times 5 \\\\
&= \dfrac {40505 \times 5} {25} \\\\
&= \dfrac {8101 \times 5 \times 5} {5 \times 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {8101 \times \cancel {5} \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5} \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
&= 8101
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-AF6C30D3-937C-4AE9-9C40-40B451DBE47C'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Work out $(57 + 56 + 55 + 54) - (56 + 55 + 54 + 53)$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= (57 + 56 + 55 + 54) - (56 + 55 + 54 + 53) \\\\
&= 57 + 56 + 55 + 54 - 56 - 55 - 54 - 53 \\\\
&= 57 + (56 - 56 + 55 - 55 + 54 - 54) - 53 \\\\
&= 57 - 53 \\\\
&= 4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= (57 + 56 + 55 + 54) - (56 + 55 + 54 + 53) \\\\
&= 57 + 56 + 55 + 54 - 56 - 55 - 54 - 53 \\\\
&= 57 + (56 - 56 + 55 - 55 + 54 - 54) - 53 \\\\
&= 57 - 53 \\\\
&= 4
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-0DB24821-F222-4AA3-A90B-8A08F41EC2D7'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

As part of a mathematics project a class is asked to look up the lengths of objects on the internet.

These objects are listed in the rectangles below and the measurements are given in the triangles.

Unfortunately these objects and measurements have been muddled up.

Draw lines on the diagram to match each object with its correct length.

![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


Width of Wembley Stadium pitch
$300 \ \text{mm}$
Height of an average man
$5600 \ \text{km}$
Distance from London to New York
$135 \ \text{m}$
Length of a piece of standard $A4$ paper
$1750 \ \text{mm}$
Height of the London Eye
$8800 \ \text{m}$
Height of Mount Everest
$6900 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

|                     Object                   |     Correct Length     |
|:--------------------------------------------:|:----------------------:|
|        Width of Wembley Stadium pitch        |   $6900 \ \text{cm}$   |
|           Height of an average man           |   $1750 \ \text{mm}$   |
|       Distance from London to New York       |   $5600 \ \text{km}$   |
|   Length of a piece of standard $A4$ paper   |   $300 \ \text{mm}$    |
|           Height of the London Eye           |    $135 \ \text{m}$    |
|           Height of Mount Everest            |   $8800 \ \text{m}$    |

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Add together the following numbers giving your answer in figures:

3 million 
seventy thousand  
thirty-two thousand 
nineteen

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 3000000 + 70000 + 32000 + 19 \\\\
&= 3102019
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3102019$

</div>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}

&= 3102019
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
